class Solution:
    def isPossibleToCutPath(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> bool:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dirs = [(-1, 0), (0, -1)]
        def dfs(i: int, j: int) -> None:
            grid[i][j] = 2
        for di, dj in dirs:
            if 0 <= i + di < m and 0 <= j + dj < n and grid[i + di][j + dj] = 1: dfs(i + di, j + dj)

        dfs(m - 1, n - 1)

        dq = collections.deque([(0, 0)])
        grid[0][0] = 0
        dirs = [(1, 0), (0, 1)]
        while dq:
            l = len(dq)
            for _ in range(l):
                i, j = dq.popleft()
                if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1: return False
                for di, dj in dirs:
                    if 0 <= i + di < m and 0 <= j + dj < n and grid[i + di][j + dj] == 2: 
                        dq.append((i + di, j + dj))
                        grid[i + di][j + dj] = 0


            if len(dq) = 1 and dq[0] != (m - 1, n - 1): return True

        return True

Let's design robust code:

```
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def isPossibleToCutPath(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> bool:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        # Mark all cells from which target is reachable as 2.
        stack = [(m - 1, n - 1)]
        grid[m - 1][n - 1] = 2
        while stack:
            i, j = stack.pop()
            for di, dj in ((-1, 0), (0, -1)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n and grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                    grid[ni][nj] = 2
                    stack.append((ni, nj))
        # BFS from source over cells that can reach target.
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0)])
        grid[0][0] = 0
        while q:
            for _ in range(len(q)):
                i, j = q.popleft()
                if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                    return False
                for di, dj in ((1, 0), (0, 1)):
                    ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                    if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n and grid[ni][nj] == 2:
                        grid[ni][nj] = 0
                        q.append((ni, nj))
            if len(q) == 1 and q[0] != (m - 1, n - 1):
                return True
        return True
```

Need maybe include `from collections import deque` instead of `import collections`. The original used `collections.deque`, so import collections. We can use `from collections import deque` and `q = deque`. But to minimize, use `import collections`.

Let's ensure code syntax:

```
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def isPossibleToCutPath(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> bool:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])

        stack = [(m - 1, n - 1)]
        grid[m - 1][n - 1] = 2
        while stack:
            i, j = stack.pop()
            for di, dj in ((-1, 0), (0, -1)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n and grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                    grid[ni][nj] = 2
                    stack.append((ni, nj))

        q = collections.deque([(0, 0)])
        grid[0][0] = 0
        while q:
            for _ in range(len(q)):
                i, j = q.popleft()
                if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                    return False
                for di, dj in ((1, 0), (0, 1)):
                    ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                    if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n and grid[ni][nj] == 2:
                        grid[ni][nj] = 0
                        q.append((ni, nj))
            if len(q) == 1 and q[0] != (m - 1, n - 1):
                return True

        return True
```

Need consider if `grid[m - 1][n - 1]` might be 0? Constraint says target is 1. But if not, reverse marking sets to 2 anyway. If target 0, no path? But problem says 1. Fine.

Let's search memory of accepted code for 2556. There is a known simple solution:
```
class Solution:
    def isPossibleToCutPath(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> bool:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        def dfs(i,j):
            if i == m-1 and j == n-1: return True
            if i >= m or j >= n or grid[i][j] == 0: return False
            grid[i][j] = 0
            if dfs(i+1,j): return True
            return dfs(i,j+1)
        if not dfs(0,0): return True
        # find second path
        grid[0][0] = 1
        return not dfs(0,0)
```

Before final, let's double-check if there is a simpler fix that preserves original structure and maybe less code. The original code attempted reverse DFS but didn't recurse. We can fix by making dfs recursive:

```
def dfs(i,j):
    grid[i][j]=2
    for di,dj in dirs:
        ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
        if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n and grid[ni][nj]==1:
            dfs(ni,nj)
dfs(m-1,n-1)
```

Then BFS. This is close to original. But recursion depth could be up to 1e5, causing RecursionError. LeetCode Python recursion limit maybe 1000; product 1e5, path length m+n-2 up to 1e5? Let's ensure code is syntactically correct:

```
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def isPossibleToCutPath(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> bool:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])

        stack = [(m - 1, n - 1)]
        grid[m - 1][n - 1] = 2
        while stack:
            i, j = stack.pop()
            for di, dj in ((-1, 0), (0, -1)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n and grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                    grid[ni][nj] = 2
                    stack.append((ni, nj))

        q = collections.deque([(0, 0)])
        grid[0][0] = 0
        while q:
            for _ in range(len(q)):
                i, j = q.popleft()
                if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                    return False
                for di, dj in ((1, 0), (0, 1)):
                    ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                    if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n and grid[ni][nj] == 2:
                        grid[ni][nj] = 0
                        q.append((ni, nj))
            if len(q) == 1 and q[0] != (m - 1, n - 1):
                return True

        return True
```

Now, let's consider if there is an even simpler solution using diagonal counts that might be more obviously correct. Could implement:

```
reachable = [[False]*n for _ in range(m)]
reachable[0][0] = grid[0][0] == 1
for i in range(m):
  for j in range(n):
    if grid[i][j] and ((i==0 and j==0) or (i>0 and reachable[i-1][j]) or (j>0 and reachable[i][j-1])):
       reachable[i][j]=True
canReach = [[False]*n for _ in range(m)]
canReach[m-1][n-1] = grid[m-1][n-1]==1
for i in reversed(range(m)):
  for j in reversed(range(n)):
    if grid[i][j] and ((i==m-1 and j==n-1) or (i+1<m and canReach[i+1][j]) or (j+1<n and canReach[i][j+1])):
       canReach[i][j]=True
counts = {}
for i in range(m):
  for j in range(n):
    if reachable[i][j] and canReach[i][j]:
       counts[i+j] = counts.get(i+j,0)+1
for k in range(1, m+n-2):
  if counts.get(k,0) == 1:
     return True
return False
```

Let's maybe write final code with no comments:

```
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def isPossibleToCutPath(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> bool:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])

        stack = [(m - 1, n - 1)]
        grid[m - 1][n - 1] = 2
        while stack:
            i, j = stack.pop()
            for di, dj in ((-1, 0), (0, -1)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n and grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                    grid[ni][nj] = 2
                    stack.append((ni, nj))

        dq = collections.deque([(0, 0)])
        grid[0][0] = 0
        while dq:
            for _ in range(len(dq)):
                i, j = dq.popleft()
                if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                    return False
                for di, dj in ((1, 0), (0, 1)):
                    ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                    if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n and grid[ni][nj] == 2:
                        grid[ni][nj] = 0
                        dq.append((ni, nj))
            if len(dq) == 1 and dq[0] != (m - 1, n - 1):
                return True

        return True
```

</think>

=== RAW ANSWER ===
>
```python
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def isPossibleToCutPath(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> bool:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])

        # Mark cells from which the target is reachable.
        stack = [(m - 1, n - 1)]
        grid[m - 1][n - 1] = 2
        while stack:
            i, j = stack.pop()
            for di, dj in ((-1, 0), (0, -1)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n and grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                    grid[ni][nj] = 2
                    stack.append((ni, nj))

        # BFS from the source over cells that can still reach the target.
        dq = collections.deque([(0, 0)])
        grid[0][0] = 0
        while dq:
            for _ in range(len(dq)):
                i, j = dq.popleft()
                if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                    return False

                for di, dj in ((1, 0), (0, 1)):
                    ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                    if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n and grid[ni][nj] == 2:
                        grid[ni][nj] = 0
                        dq.append((ni, nj))

            if len(dq) == 1 and dq[0] != (m - 1, n - 1):
                return True

        return True
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def isPossibleToCutPath(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> bool:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])

        # Mark cells from which the target is reachable.
        stack = [(m - 1, n - 1)]
        grid[m - 1][n - 1] = 2
        while stack:
            i, j = stack.pop()
            for di, dj in ((-1, 0), (0, -1)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n and grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                    grid[ni][nj] = 2
                    stack.append((ni, nj))

        # BFS from the source over cells that can still reach the target.
        dq = collections.deque([(0, 0)])
        grid[0][0] = 0
        while dq:
            for _ in range(len(dq)):
                i, j = dq.popleft()
                if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                    return False

                for di, dj in ((1, 0), (0, 1)):
                    ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                    if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n and grid[ni][nj] == 2:
                        grid[ni][nj] = 0
                        dq.append((ni, nj))

            if len(dq) == 1 and dq[0] != (m - 1, n - 1):
                return True

        return True
